Farioli in conferenza stampa: “Il Napoli ha preparato la gara in maniera intelligente, match importante”

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Francesco Farioli, allenatore del Porto, ha rilasciato alcune dichiarazioni in conferenza stampa dopo la vittoria in Champions League contro il Napoli:  “Il gioco è stato molto pesante, ci sono stati diversi momenti in cui siamo stati molto energetici e coraggiosi, soprattutto quando siamo andati a prendere la palla e loro ci pressavano, avevamo la giusta mentalità come i tifosi e questo ci ha aiutato ad avere la prestazione avuta. In ogni intervista ho detto che è stata una buona Champions ma non avevamo avuto punti, stavolta abbiamo raccolto i frutti. Questa vittoria è importante perchè ci servivano punti, sappiamo la storia del club e l’esigenza di fare bene in Champions League, di ottenere risultati. Una cosa non si può dire, che ci sia mancanza di volontà: ogni tre giorni abbiamo le giuste motivazioni, ci sono sempre gli avversari e le partite da giocare, e non sempre siamo al top della forma e a certi livelli la pressione si fa sentire. Dobbiamo imparare sulla nostra pelle, ci prendiamo i tre punti contro una squadra eccellente e ora vediamo le ultime quattro partite per guadagnare più punti possibili. La partita di stasera e i risultati hanno cambiato la prospettiva del match, abbiamo dovuto cambiare interpretazione del match e il coraggio dei ragazzi è stato notevole. Siamo stati bravissimi nella verticalizzazione e nel secondo tempo, l’impatto delle sostituzioni è stato fondamentale, chi è entrato ha contributo tanto. Cosa non è andato bene alla fine del primo tempo? In Champions ogni punto conta così come ogni gol può fare la differenza, stasera vincere con tre gol di differenza sicuramente è importante e ci aiuta ad avanzare, la performance dei ragazzi è stata eccellente. Le difficoltà del primo tempo? Non sapevamo cosa avrebbero fatto, avevamo preparato cinque scenari possibili e stasera abbiamo visto le scelte degli avversari con giocatori che non ci aspettavamo in campo. Abbiamo reagito bene e fatto la differenza. Come ho visto il Napoli? Non per dare meriti ai miei, ma il Napoli ha preparato la partita in maniera intelligente, quando ho visto la formazione ho pensato come potessero creare. La prima lettura è stata una scelta strategica e ben pensata in pochi minuti, abbiamo cercato di aggiustare le nuove dinamiche con l’ingresso di De Bruyne sotto punta, siamo stati bravi a portare dentro tanta energia, nel secondo tempo siamo venuti fuori con la condizione fisica e ritmo, chi è entrato ha alzato il ritmo ed è qualcosa in cui credo molto. Ho chiesto a Fofana e Gimenez di giocare fuoriruolo ma hanno avuto determinazione e spirito. Il calcio italiano? Le informazioni sono veloci e si può raffrontarsi con altre realtà, la Serie A la seguo da tifoso e da osservatore esterno, alcune dinamiche le conoscete meglio voi. Nuova onda degli allenatori toscani? Facciamo un lavoro bellissimo quando i risultati vanno bene, difficilissimo se la palla non entra. Spalletti ed Allegri sono dei riferimenti del calcio italiano, hanno portato innovazione nel ruolo dell’allenatore e ho un rispetto enorme per ciò che hanno fatto e continuano a fare. Su Alvini posso dire che è incredibile per lo spirito che ha il Frosinone, tanti anni fa andavo a vedere i suoi allenamenti al Tuttocuoio e ha sempre avuto una grande passione. Mi ha aperto le porte del suo centro sportivo per gli allenamenti e ancora non pensavo di allenare, ha un modo contagioso per come allena. Come abbiamo vinto? A fine primo tempo ho detto di avere ancora fiducia come nei primi 35′ di partita. Abbiamo avuto coraggio e alla lunga abbiamo meritato la vittoria, grande meriti ai ragazzi. Gabri Veiga aveva la febbre, aveva una faccia bianca stamattina e non aveva dormito: quando è uscito gli ho detto meglio fare così ogni partita. Ha grande intelligenza, conosce i momenti della gara e oltre a leggere il gioco aggiunge qualcosa di speciale come la sua fisicità strabordante come volume ed esplosività. Ha ancora grandi margini di miglioramento sulla continuità, sta migliorando e rispetto al suo percorso ha voglia di fare tanto e bene. Poi tra cinque anni chissà, per ora ce lo godiamo. Occasione per un italiano all’estero prima di tornare in Italia? A volte non danno e a volte non si prendono le opportunità, ci sono momenti: negli ultimi due anni e mezzo ho lavorato per club con sale piene di trofei. Lavorare assieme come succede al Porto poi ti rende difficile guardare altrove, sono felice qui: è la realtà, sono felice di lavorare con un gruppo straordinario e con le persone che rappresentano il club e fanno di tutto per il club, creativi nel cercare soluzioni nei momenti di difficoltà. Abbiamo tante partite da giocare, testa bassa e pedalare”.

What Wall Street billionaires are saying about Democrats' midterm sweep

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

America's billionaires woke up Wednesday to a political reckoning — and a Wall Street rally.

As Democrats celebrated a sweeping midterm victory, some of the country's wealthiest donors traded barbs over record election spending. Others warned the results could threaten the artificial intelligence boom.

Wall Street, however, cheered the outcome. The Dow Jones Industrial Average soared 1,569 points, or 3%, while the S&P 500 gained 1.6% and the Nasdaq Composite rose 1.4%, as investors welcomed political gridlock, reduced uncertainty and President Donald Trump's willingness to accept the results.

Virgin Group founder Richard Branson took aim at the billions spent influencing the election.

"I have felt embarrassed by the billions that tech entrepreneurs and hedge fund managers have spent," Branson wrote Wednesday on LinkedIn, calling for campaign finance reform. Branson did not give donations that met the threshold reporting in the 2026 election cycle.

His comments came as Elon Musk, the election's largest individual donor, watched his preferred Republicans lose the Texas Senate and governor races despite pouring $224 million into the state — nearly half his record $474 million in political giving nationwide, according to a CNBC analysis of campaign finance records.

Early Wednesday, the Tesla and SpaceX CEO suggested on X that data centers in space were looking increasingly appealing before joking that his "election-interfering space lasers" had failed.

LinkedIn co-founder Reid Hoffman, another major political donor, took a sharper tone.

"We should get money out of politics," Hoffman wrote late Tuesday on X, accusing Musk of spending "outrageous amounts to keep authoritarian clowns in power" while defending his own contributions to Democrats.

Other Democratic megadonors celebrated. "The world changed yesterday," Alex Soros wrote Wednesday on X, hailing the results as a generational victory after his family contributed roughly $103 million to Democratic political efforts.

But beyond the political sparring, some of the country's richest investors were focused on a potentially bigger consequence of the Democratic sweep: the future of the AI boom.

Even before polls closed Tuesday, Pershing Square founder Bill Ackman warned on CNBC's "Squawk on the Street" that slowing AI development could jeopardize America's technological edge. "Practically speaking, we can't slow down," he said.

As results rolled in Tuesday night, billionaire investor Chamath Palihapitiya sounded an even starker warning.

Palihapitiya initially questioned whether the early results would hold, but warned that if they did, the implications for the AI trade could be severe.

"And the only way to sink the AI trade is to sink the data centers," Palihapitiya said during an election-night livestream of the "All-In" podcast.

Texas offered an early test of those fears. Democratic Gov.-elect Gina Hinojosa, who has proposed a moratorium on new data-center construction until stronger safeguards are established, defeated three-term Republican incumbent Greg Abbott.

During the same livestream, Altimeter Capital CEO Brad Gerstner warned the Democratic wave could mean at least 10 to 15 gigawatts of expected AI data-center capacity would not come online in 2027. Wall Street analyst Dan Ives estimated Wednesday that 10% of planned U.S. data centers would no longer go ahead.

AMD CEO Lisa Su acknowledged the growing resistance to AI's expansion, urging technology companies to demonstrate the benefits of data centers to local communities.

"At the end of the day, what we need to do is ensure that everybody wins from AI," Su told CNBC Wednesday.

Yet Wall Street may have found opportunity in the same slowdown that worried AI's biggest backers.

Fewer new data centers could increase the value of existing facilities while easing the enormous spending burden on technology giants. Big Tech stocks surged Wednesday, adding more than $750 billion in market value at one point, even as smaller, debt-dependent data-center operators tumbled.

Jackson Olson Jokes About Impressing GF Maggie's Dad Pat Sajak on Vacation

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Savannah Bananas star Jackson Olson is winning over the entire Sajak family — one TikTok at a time.

“First trip with the Sajak fam = success … I think?” Olson, 29, wrote via TikTok on Tuesday, November 3, alongside footage with girlfriend Maggie Sajak and her famous dad, former Wheel of Fortune host Pat Sajak, lip-syncing a beloved Gilmore Girls scene.

“What do you have to offer?” Pat, 80, mouthed before Olson replied, “Nothing, only this,” and started dancing. (Sean Gunn’s Kirk Gleason originally said the now-viral “nothing” line in his character’s avant-garde short film, which premiered during a town movie night during season 2.)

After receiving a look from Pat, the Dancing With the Stars season 35 castoff buried his face in his hands and walked off camera.

Olson has been dating Maggie, 31, since April.

“It’s really cool because we’re obviously living very different lives right now on the opposite side of the country, but we’re able to come together and just have an awesome relationship,” Olson exclusively told Us in May. “I feel like in any relationship, you’re trying to figure out how to see each other as frequently as possible, which is never something that I thought in my past I was going to want to do … but now I really do.”

He continued at the time, “It’s just about planning and making sure you’re setting aside time to see each other and make really cool experiences happen because a start of a relationship never happens again. You only get one chance at a start of a relationship.”

While speaking with Us, Olson revealed that he already met Pat.

“He’s a great guy,” he gushed. “Everybody’s nervous to meet the father of the person they’re dating, or mother or both. I’m a very weird person, I’m a very out there person. I’m not going to hold back who I am as a person, and people seem to enjoy that company. I’m happy that I’m able to be myself truly in every aspect now.”

Olson also had Maggie’s support during his DWTS tenure.

“It’s so awesome [to have her here]. I try not to look at her during the dance because I know that she’s smiling,” he told Us in September, weeks before his Halloween Horror Nights elimination. “I know that she’s so excited and smiling. And if I looked at her, I would just like be like, ‘Ah!’ But it’s awesome, and she’s so supportive of both of us, too. She was wearing the Team Banana ballroom shirt today, so that was cool.”

The Blue Wave Has Tech Bros Scrambling for Data Centers in Space

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

A wave of Democratic candidates across the country—and notably in Texas, a national data center hot spot and home to Elon Musk’s SpaceX—won their state and congressional races on Tuesday, including many candidates who voiced opposition to the data center industry. The results have sent some corners of the tech industry looking to the heavens for answers.

In recent hours, data center enthusiasts have proclaimed on social media that they’re still “bullish” on building data centers—just not necessarily in Texas, or even on Earth at all. Instead, they say, it’s time to focus on data centers in space.

Elon Musk—who spent more than $400 million of his fortune supporting Republican candidates who all went on to lose their midterms races—has been among those leading the charge. “Data centers in space looking pretty appealing right now,” Musk posted on X in the early hours of Wednesday, after it was clear that Democrats had taken the House and Senate.

Jordan Lasker—the man behind the X account "Crémieux,” which is a popular follow in the tech world and is known for posting race-based pseudoscience—also weighed in, saying, “Data centers in space are an incredible idea and anyone who doubted them didn't think enough about tonight's election results.”

Throughout the day, Musk employees, tech investors, AI researchers, venture capitalists, and industry fans joined the chorus of support for space-based data centers. Soham Mehta, a research fellow at the techno-optimist think tank Foundation for American Innovation, argued that underwater data centers should also be considered. (In June, China began operating what it claims is the world’s first underwater data center.)

Some companies are already trying to put compute in orbit. Among them are Google, which launched a satellite that it says contains “data center class TPU,” and SpaceX, which says it aims to deploy “thousands of AI satellites starting as soon as late 2027.” Blue Origin is also in the mix, having filed for a Federal Communications Commission permit to launch 50,000 satellites that will constitute an “orbital data center system.”

There are also a few startups aiming to build data centers in space, including Starcloud, which claims to have carried "the first Nvidia H100 GPU into orbit" in November 2025.

Despite being technically possible, the realities of in-orbit data centers are difficult. Among other things, space-based data centers are much more expensive than land-based ones, can’t grow to hyperscale size, and are subject to the risks and whims of satellite launches.

When it comes to Texas data centers, the concerns of tech bros aren’t completely unfounded. Experts tell WIRED that the change in leadership could translate to some concrete changes in the industry—albeit with limits.

Although Democrats will have control of the Texas House of Representatives, Republicans still have control over the state’s senate. Robert Paterson, professor of land use and environmental planning at the University of Texas at Austin, tells WIRED that the split means that some immediate attempts at change “will probably be stymied a bit.”

Adrian Shelley, director of nonprofit consumer advocacy group Public Citizen’s Texas office, tells WIRED that the legislature would be a likely place to hash out high-impact policies like the reallocation of state agency resources. However, he adds that the governor could effect more immediate changes by issuing executive orders, or requesting the resignation of politically appointed officials in state agencies.

Shelley says that these oversight agencies—such as the Texas Commission on Environmental Quality, the Public Utility Commission, and the Texas Water Development Board—have the authority to charge fines for breaking state law, or in extreme cases to revoke permits for projects like data centers. But Paterson notes that Texas developers have particularly strong “vested rights,” which protects the terms of their projects from the moment their permits are approved, even if construction hasn’t begun.

Another medium-term route to reshaping Texas’ data center industry could be to give more power to its counties. Paterson noted that nearly half of the state’s data centers exist in unincorporated territory, which puts them outside the jurisdiction of city zoning laws.

Unlike other states, Paterson says, Texas counties have relatively limited enforcement abilities. Under the state's so-called Dillon’s Rule, counties only have powers that are “specifically allotted to it by the state.” So while they have authority over things like access and egress to county roads or construction on floodplains, he says they don't have the zoning authority that would help closely regulate projects like data centers.

Paterson says that the new attorney general could theoretically adjust the terms in the state’s biannual handbook, which establishes the limits of county authority. He also argues that even in a split legislature, especially since anti-data-center sentiment crosses party lines, state lawmakers could propose a viable bill giving counties “limited zoning authority over data centers."

Shelley tells WIRED that since “data centers were a decisive factor in this blue wave,” the upcoming legislative season will be “very interesting.”

“I will say the data center industry hasn't shown a lot of interest in working with state lawmakers or local leaders,” Shelley says, “and I will be interested to see if that changes.”

Yoshitomo Nara Is Gen Z's Favorite Artist—Even if They Don't Know His Name

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Four years ago, when Terrell Brown was a college student in Wheaton, Illinois, he spotted an oversized ashtray at a local Goodwill. It featured a cartoon-like illustration of a fierce-looking girl smoking a cigarette, along with the words “Too Young to Die.” It cost him $10. Brown recognized the imagery but didn’t know the name of the artist who created it. But when he posted a photo on Reddit a reader commented, “Yoshitomo Nara is a really cool and famous artist, this is an amazing find!”

Brown, now a vintage reseller with almost 360,000 followers on TikTok, resold the ashtray, still in its original packaging, on eBay for $2,860. The episode says something about the powerful attraction—and afterlife—of Nara’s imagery. The artist’s signature figures—round-headed children with enormous eyes and expressions that oscillate between mischief, defiance, and melancholy—are deceptively simple. They often feature slogans like, “No nukes” and “Rock ‘n’ roll is not dead.”

Fans include the world’s wealthiest—like Salesforce founder Marc Benioff, a longtime Yoshitomo Nara collector who supported a major retrospective of Nara’s work last year at London’s Hayward Gallery, co-organized by the Guggenheim Museum Bilbao and Museum Frieder Burda in Baden-Baden. His work has also become something of a fixture in homes featured in AD, from the Nara sculpture in Usher’s backyard to the painting hanging in Kris Jenner’s dining room. But they also include Gen Zers who love the characters without necessarily knowing much about the artist behind them. Nara’s artwork has attained a kind of cult currency, circulating through dorm rooms, online marketplaces, thrift stores, and social media, the characters becoming so familiar they seem to belong to everyone.

Stella McCartney x Yoshitomo Nara handbag

This week, as powerhouse art dealer David Zwirner unveils the first major solo exhibition of Nara’s work in New York in over a decade (his first at the gallery since leaving Pace last year) that duality is especially striking. The highly anticipated show is poised to be a museum-quality affair. Curated by Nara himself, it will feature paintings, drawings, and sculptures across the gallery’s two Chelsea locations. Yet the gallery will undoubtedly also draw a crowd of broader Nara fans—the public who adore his instantly recognizable characters, even if they know them best as decorative icons closer to the cultural territory of Hello Kitty or Miffy.

Now in his 60s and based in Tochigi Prefecture, about 100 miles north of Tokyo, Nara emerged on the international art scene in the 1990s. His creations’ simultaneously innocent and world-weary affect is central to their appeal. The characters “look cute on the surface,” says Takako Nagasawa, deputy chairwoman at Phillips auction house, “but if you read into them, they’re not cute, like the girl who has a knife hidden behind her back.” Many collectors, she notes, display Nara’s work in their children’s bedrooms. “I think that two-sidedness is the attraction, and very relevant now. We want to talk about what’s going on around the world, but we also don’t want to talk about it.”

Usher and wife Jennifer Goicoechea Raymond, in front of Yoshitomo Nara’s Peace Head in their garden.

Usher's daughter's room, which showcases two Nara artworks

Yoshitomo Nara, Matryoshka, 2026. © Yoshitomo Nara 2026, courtesy of the artist and David Zwirner

Yoshitomo Nara painting in a home published in July 2013 issue of AD

Nara prizes accessibility to his art and has actively participated in extending his imagery into everyday products. While other artists have created characters that appear on merchandise, like Kaws’s Companion and Jeff Koons’s balloon dog, none have endured and proliferated quite like Nara’s, which have an underlying rebelliousness that speaks to disaffected youth across generations. Yoshiki, the frontman of the rock band X Japan, is working with Nara on the packaging for his Napa Valley wines, Y by Yoshiki, adorned with the artist’s characters. Stella McCartney, a close friend of Nara’s, has previewed her third limited-edition collaboration with the artist, ranging from $100 shoelaces to a $7,900 knit scarf. The merchandise Nara designs often connects to themes in his art, like the $12 band-aids, $85 snow globes, and $1,200 table lamps available at the MoMA Design Store. (He is one of the most searched artists on MoMA’s website, says Chay Costello, the museum’s associate director of merchandising and product development.)

Sweaters from Stella McCartney’s third collaboration with Yoshitomo Nara

Stella McCartney x Yoshitomo Nara collection will be available starting November 11

Content creator Nick Lowry, who bought one of Nara’s ashtrays 20 years ago on eBay for $250, posted a TikTok video a few months ago of himself removing the object from its longtime spot on his coffee table and hanging it on the wall. (The original acrylic painting on which the ashtray is based is in the collection of the Rubell Museum, in Miami.) “Objects like this are one of the only ways to own work by a living artist whose paintings now sell for more than I could ever afford,” Lowry says in the video, “so it deserved a better spot.”

Benioff describes Nara as “one of the most important artists of our time.” Like many others, Benioff came to Nara’s work through eBay. He purchased journals designed by the artist and his “little books” about the Zen Buddhist concept of Shoshin, commonly translated as “beginner’s mind.” Benioff, who met Nara through Yoshiki, now considers the artist a close friend. He owns multiple artworks by the artist, including a drawing of a grimacing girl that reads “I’m just alright,” a totemic fiberglass sculpture of two heads in a bowl, and a monumental bronze head. “It’s great that I picked them up early,” he says of the pieces, “It's really almost impossible to get anything [on the primary market]. The best pieces all go to the museums.”

Yoshitomo Nara painting in a home published in January 2018 AD

Yoshitomo Nara, The Little Pilgrims (Night Walking), up for auction this week at Sotheby's

Numerous Nara works are coming to auction in conjunction with the opening of the Zwirner show. (Nara’s auction record is $25 million for his seven-and-a-half-foot canvas, Knife Behind Back, sold at Sotheby’s Hong Kong in 2019.) His eight-and-a-half-foot painting Milky Lake / Thinking One, featuring a little girl with downcast eyes, is coming up for sale later this month at the online auction platform Fair Warning at an estimate of $10 million. Next week, Christie’s is offering an eight-foot high bronze version of his character, Miss Forest, for $1 million in its 21st Century Evening Sale and Sotheby’s is selling his painting Candy Blue Night, from 2001, at an estimate of $5 million, and a set of fiberglass sculptures, The Little Pilgrims (Night Walking), from a 1999 edition, at an estimate of $1.2 million. (The sellers, Theodore and Ruth Baum, bought the set at auction two decades ago for just $360,000.)

For the Zwirner show, Nara has paired works from his student days in Düsseldorf in the 1980s and 1990s with recent ones, demonstrating how he incorporated drawing into his practice throughout his career. He’s also exhibiting 13 oversized bronze heads, enlarged from palm-sized clay models inspired by the snow “monsters” from his childhood winters in northern rural Japan. The show coincides with the reopening last week of the Hirshhorn Sculpture Garden in Washington, D.C. after a four-year renovation, where three larger aluminum versions of the heads are on view.

Nara sculptures at the reopened Hirshhorn Sculpture Garden in Washington, D.C.

For most artists, the distance between the audiences for Nara’s masterpieces and his mass-produced products would be difficult to bridge. But for Nara, it may be the very thing that makes him so compelling. His work can be both a million-dollar trophy and a teenager’s trinket. And while the Zwirner show draws the art world’s big collectors and curators, the next generation of fans may be discovering him somewhere else entirely: on TikTok, Instagram, or in a gift shop.

Yoshitomo Nara bandages sold at MoMA Design Store

Andoni Iraola blasts Liverpool stars for 'cheap and messy' defeat at Fenerbahce - and blames the NOISE inside stadium for second goal as Turkey curse continues

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Published: 18:10 EDT, 4 November 2026 | Updated: 19:11 EDT, 4 November 2026

Liverpool boss Andoni Iraola blasted his ‘cheap and messy’ side after they fell to a 3-2 defeat at Fenerbahce.

Former cult hero Dirk Kuyt was in the dugout as two Englishmen – Mason Greenwood and Archie Brown – scored for the Turkish giants to cancel out Trey Nyoni and Alexander Isak’s strikes before Vedat Muriqi headed home a winner.

All three goals were avoidable but may have been due to the deafening atmosphere and Iraola said: ‘(They were) cheap goals. Messy goals.

‘The free-kick is quite far out, through the wall. The last goal was a long throw, the second one Cody (Gakpo) touched the ball just in front of Alisson.

‘In the stadium it was almost impossible to communicate, it was difficult for me to give instructions and the communication was difficult between Cody and Ali.’

Iraola also stressed his frustration with his team conceding three from set-pieces, saying: 'It's something that we were doing very well in the league, we were doing quite well until today.

'We knew it was one of the strengths of Fenerbahce. We concede from a free-kick, a corner and even from the long throw at the end.'

Tonight's loss was Liverpool's second in as many games but first in the Champions League this season

Vedat Muriqi's 79th minute header sealed the victory for Fenerbahce

Liverpool full-back Kostas Tsimikas also had some criticisms of the Reds defensive performance as he said: 'We didn't defend after the goal we conceded. After three or four minutes, automatically, we conceded another goal. I think we have to mark better if we want to win games.'

Meanwhile Kuyt, who played 285 times for the Reds, said: ‘It was a very special night for everyone who loves Fenerbahce. I have to give a very big compliment to the players and the staff who keep pushing them.

‘We told each other to stick together and be united. That’s something I learned from Liverpool, who we played against. We made a miracle tonight, not many expected us to win.’

It was Liverpool’s second straight defeat after the 1-0 reverse at home to Arsenal and the third Champions League game on the bounce where they have conceded three goals.

The result was also the third defeat in Istanbul in 13 months, extending Liverpool's miserable run in Turkey since their 2005 Champions League final heroics.

Iraola's side travel to Crystal Palace on Sunday.

Champions League: Como clobber Lens 9-1 after Real Madrid roar back to deny AEK Athens

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Valverde hits late winner as Madrid win 3-2 in Athens

Napoli thrashed by Porto, Club Brugge win at PSV

Anastasios Douvikas led the rout with a hat-trick as Como cruised to a 9-1 victory at Lens, equalling the record for the most goals scored in a single Champions League game.

Only two teams had previously won a game by a margin of eight goals since the launch of the modern Champions League in 1992. Liverpool thumped Besiktas 8-0 in the group stage in 2007 and Real Madrid won by the same scoreline against Malmo in 2015.

Como are just the second team to score nine times in a game in the competition, after Bayern Munich beat Dinamo Zagreb 9-2 in the league phase in 2024.

Cesc Fàbregas’s side made it three wins from four in their maiden campaign in the competition with a spellbinding display.

Nico Paz continued his fine form with a goal and two assists, while Lucas Da Cunha, Marc-Oliver Kempf, Mattia Liberali, Martin Baturina and Matteo Papaccioli also scored for the Serie A side, who had already beaten both RB Leipzig and Manchester United 4-1 at home in this campaign.

The last club to score nine away from home in the European Cup was Feyenoord against Olympiakos in 1971.

Federico Valverde lashed home a stunning effort from distance to snatch Real Madrid a late 3-2 victory at AEK Athens on Wednesday and preserve their 100% start in the group phase.

José Mourinho’s side, who started with Vinícius Júnior on the left flank after he missed the team’s previous two La Liga matches through suspension, twice came from behind against the Greek champions before Valverde claimed all three points for his side.

Aboubakary Koïta sent AEK ahead after 12 minutes before Jude Bellingham equalised with a sublime flick for the record 15-time winners.

Stavros Pilios sent the Greek champions ahead with 15 minutes to go but Kylian Mbappé swiftly pulled Madrid level once again, setting the stage for Valverde’s late winner.

Madrid have now won seven successive games across all competitions.

Scott McTominay found the net but Napoli endured a 4-1 defeat by Porto, who had lost their first three games in the competition, while Club Brugge are up to ninth after a 2-1 win at PSV.

Konstantinos Karetsas struck a 72nd-minute winner as Borussia Dortmund beat Real Betis 2-1, while Erik Botheim helped himself to two goals as Viking secured their first triumph with a 4-1 win against 10-man Sabah.

Why Is John Malkovich’s New Movie Called Wild Horse Nine? The Answer Is Not What I Expected

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Join The Club for quick access. Enter your email below and we'll send confirmation, and sign you up to our newsletter.

I Didn't Expect An Animated Movie To Be One Of The Scariest Films I've Seen In 2026 (So Far)

I Wondered Why Nathan Fielder Was Picked For You Can See Everything. His Answer Confused Me

I'm Shocked To Learn One Surprising Scene In You Can See Everything Came From Billy Evans Himself

Unlock instant access to exclusive member features.

Get full access to premium articles, exclusive features and a growing list of member rewards.

Well, I didn't expect this answer from Martin McDonagh.

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

Before I walked into my screening of Wild Horse Nine, a big question I had was: Why on Earth is it called Wild Horse Nine? When I walked out of the film starring John Malkovich and Sam Rockwell, I was still pondering that question. So, when I had the chance to interview Martin McDonagh, the writer/director of the project on the 2026 movie schedule, I asked him about the meaning behind his film’s title. I have to say, his answer is not what I expected.

Knowing that Wild Horse Nine follows two CIA agents, Malkovich’s Chris and Rockwell’s Lee, as they travel to Easter Island in the ‘70s, I figured the movie’s title could be related to their mission. I thought Wild Horse Nine could be a code for something. However, that was totally wrong, because when I asked Martin McDonagh why he gave his film this title, he told me:

Yeah, I've got no idea. I like numbers, and I like horses.

Well, there you have it, there’s really no rhyme or reason to the name. McDonagh just likes numbers and horses, and that’s all there is to it, according to him. That's certainly not what I expected to hear when I asked the question.

However, the title is by no means completely meaningless. Wild horses are important to the film, and they come up often, especially in relation to Chris’s journey. So, that part makes sense. However, the number nine is totally random…as far as I and McDonagh can tell.

This is also quite funny because many of his movies have straightforward names. Three Billboards Outside Ebbing, Missouri, which Sam Rockwell won his Oscar for, is about just that: three billboards outside Ebbing, Missouri. The beloved Seven Psychopaths is about a man writing a screenplay with the same name, and it’s also about psychopaths literally. In Bruges takes place in Bruges, Belgium. And the great 2022 comedy The Banshees of Inisherin is set in the fictional Inisherin, banshees and their existence are discussed, and there is a song of the same name that’s talked about too.

At no point in Wild Horse Nine are the words said together, it’s for sure not a CIA thing, and it left me very curious about why McDonagh named it that. Personally, I love that he just did it for no big reason; he simply liked numbers and horses. From my point of view, it also just sounds really cool.

The movie is very cool, slick, funny and deeply tragic too. To me, John Malkovich gives a performance so incredible that Wild Horse Nine is bound to go down as one of his best movies. Rockwell beautifully balances him out, too, and further proves why he’s such a great fit for McDonagh’s writing – seeing as this is the third movie they’ve worked on together. Parker Posey, Steve Buscemi and Mariana di Girolamo round out the cast of this dark comedy, and like the leads, they also play out the laugh-out-loud comedy brilliantly.

So, if you are looking to see Wild Horse Nine, you can catch it in theaters on November 6. It’s one wild, funny and thought-provoking ride. However, don’t think too hard about its name, because that really doesn’t mean anything.

Riley Utley is the Weekend Editor at CinemaBlend. She graduated with her master’s degree in arts journalism and communications from the Newhouse School at Syracuse University. Since joining the CB team she has covered numerous TV shows and movies -- including her personal favorite shows Ted Lasso and The Marvelous Mrs. Maisel. She also has followed and consistently written about everything from Taylor Swift to Fire Country, and she's enjoyed every second of it.

On the Couch with Christopher John Rogers

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

In honor of his new collaboration with The Rug Company, the fashion designer shares his inspirations and style icons.

Every item on this page was chosen by an ELLE Decor editor. We may earn commission on some of the items you choose to buy.

Christopher John Rogers has an eye for color. The fashion designer, born in Baton Rouge and based in New York, established his self-titled label in 2016. Over the past decade, he’s become known for his use of bold tones and voluminous silhouettes, and for dressing the likes of Michelle Obama and Cardi B. Now, Rogers is taking his style to the floor in a new collaboration with The Rug Company.

The collection, which Rogers designed with Will Cooper, creative director of The Rug Company, feels alive, with vibrant, swooshing marks, multicolored doodles in wool and silk, and wavy threads that appear to dance across the floor. “The scribbles, the brushstrokes, the happy accidents, the lines that don't quite meet,” Rogers tells ELLE Decor. “I love the idea of taking something spontaneous and seemingly incidental and rendering it permanent through this incredibly meticulous, centuries-old craft.”

Launching tomorrow, the collaboration includes six styles across ten colorways. But Rogers has more planned beyond womenswear, “exploring new ways for the brand to exist beyond the wardrobe,” he says. “I'm particularly excited by opportunities that allow us to think about color, proportion, texture, and materiality through different disciplines, whether that's furniture, interiors, or something entirely unexpected.”

Below, Rogers takes ELLE Decor’s On the Couch survey, sharing a few of his favorite pieces, destinations, and entertaining takes.

Favorite Sofa: Pierre Paulin's ABCD sofa for Artifort. The color theory is delicious, and I'd happily sacrifice a little comfort for something that fabulous.

Architect You Admire: Ettore Sottsass. I love the clarity of his vision and his ability to make the simplest shapes feel monumental.

Destination Museum: The Louisiana Museum of Modern Art in Denmark

Go-to Galleries: 52 Walker, Broadway Gallery, and Salon 94 in New York.

Favorite Artist/Artwork: I've recently fallen for Domenica Brockman. We have one of her paintings in the studio, and I'm constantly struck by how much emotion she can communicate through deceptively simple arrangements of color.

Vintage Piece of Your Dreams: An original Verner Panton Living Tower.

Hotels to Check Into: Hotel Saint Vincent in New Orleans, and La Mamounia in Marrakech.

Entertaining Tips: Great lighting, a boozy cocktail, and a playlist with a point of view. Don't overthink it!

Style Icons: Bert and Ernie, and Isaac Mizrahi.

Required Reading: Interaction of Color by Josef Albers and The Bluest Eye by Toni Morrison. Two very different but equally essential ways of learning to see.

Cocktail or Mocktail of Choice: It's a toss-up between a wet Roku martini and a Cynar spritz.

Favorite Restaurant: Cervo’s in the Lower East Side, Rolo's in Ridgewood, Frenchette in Tribeca, and Santa Panza in Bushwick.

Favorite Jeweler: Presley Oldham and Bernard James for contemporary jewelry, and vintage Alexander Calder or Alber Elbaz-era Lanvin for something a little more fantastical.

Annie Goldsmith is the senior editor and digital lead at ELLE Decor, where she covers design, culture, style, and trends. She previously held positions at The Information, covering technology and culture, and Town & Country, writing about news, entertainment, and fashion. Her work has also appeared in Vogue, Rolling Stone, and the SF Standard.

Ill-Fated Learjet Had Altitude Struggles Amid Severe Turbulence 6 Months Ago, ATC Audio Shows

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

The Learjet 60 that crashed in Florida on Sunday, killing all nine people onboard, had experienced a troubling encounter with severe turbulence just six months earlier. Resurfaced air traffic control audio from May 2026 reveals that the same aircraft struggled to maintain its assigned altitude near Newark, New Jersey, prompting a controller to challenge the crew's description of the conditions as merely moderate turbulence.

The revelation, originally reported by the Daily Express, raises questions about similarities between the two incidents, particularly in how the pilots characterized the turbulence they encountered. Before Sunday's fatal crash, the Learjet's crew also reported moderate turbulence despite altitude fluctuations of up to 700 feet. However, investigators have not established a connection between the incidents, and the identities of the pilots involved in the May incident remain unconfirmed.

The earlier encounter occurred in May, when the Learjet, registered as N722BD, was flying near Newark Liberty International Airport (EWR). According to audio obtained by The US Sun, a controller noticed the business jet climbing unexpectedly and questioned the crew. A female pilot responded that they were "just about to call you," said they were experiencing moderate turbulence, and were unable to maintain their assigned altitude.

The controller immediately challenged that assessment and instructed the pilots to descend to 4,000 feet. The situation was particularly concerning because another business jet was operating nearby at a similar altitude, potentially creating a separation issue. A male pilot subsequently reiterated that the Learjet was experiencing moderate chop, but the controller insisted that an inability to maintain altitude indicated more serious conditions.

"If you can't maintain altitude, that's not moderate. That's severe turbulence."

The controller then explicitly asked whether the aircraft was encountering severe turbulence, prompting affirmative responses from both pilots. When questioned about possible injuries, the female pilot reported none, but confirmed that severe turbulence was continuing. The identities of the two pilots have not been established, and there is no confirmation that either was aboard the aircraft during its fatal flight six months later.

The May exchange highlights an important distinction in how pilots report turbulence. Under the Federal Aviation Administration's (FAA) turbulence classifications, moderate turbulence can produce changes in altitude, attitude, and airspeed, but pilots should retain positive control. Severe turbulence involves larger, more abrupt changes and can leave an aircraft momentarily out of position. The controller's concern was therefore consistent with established aviation terminology.

Slight, irregular changes in altitude or attitude

Changes in altitude or attitude, with positive control maintained

Large, abrupt changes; aircraft may be momentarily out of position

Aircraft violently tossed about; control practically impossible

This distinction matters in congested airspace, where aircraft must maintain assigned altitudes to preserve separation from other traffic. An unexpected climb or descent can create a potential conflict even when the deviation is caused by atmospheric conditions rather than pilot action. Accurate turbulence reports also help controllers warn following aircraft and coordinate alternative altitudes. However, difficulty maintaining altitude does not automatically establish a mechanical problem or loss of control.

For N722BD, the May recording indicates that the aircraft deviated up to 700 feet above and 400 feet below its assigned altitude of 4,000 feet. That suggests that the initial description of a "moderate chop" significantly understated the difficulties the crew was experiencing, and supports the controller's assessment of severe turbulence.

This article serves as reassurance that turbulence rarely poses a danger to passengers and requires little effort from pilots to navigate.

N722BD departed Austin Bergstrom International Airport (AUS), bound for North Eleuthera Airport (ELH) in the Bahamas, carrying seven passengers and two pilots. While crossing South Florida at 35,000 feet, the crew reported light clear icing and turbulence, with altitude deviations of approximately 600-700 feet. The similarities to May are striking: once again, pilots aboard the same aircraft described "moderate turbulence" while experiencing substantial altitude fluctuations. Nevertheless, those fluctuations alone do not prove that the November conditions met the FAA's severe classification.

After encountering precipitation, the crew requested permission to climb to 37,000 feet, hoping to find smoother conditions. ATC approved the request, but less than 30 seconds later, the pilots reported an uncontrolled descent and declared an emergency. According to Flightradar24, the aircraft's reported descent rate exceeded 32,000 feet per minute before it crashed in Palm Beach County, killing everyone aboard.

The NTSB investigation has already uncovered evidence of an inflight breakup, including pieces of the aircraft's left elevator found between 1.3 miles (2.1 km) and 3.8 miles (6.1 km) from the impact site. Investigators are examining weather, aircraft systems, maintenance records, and flight communications. Although both encounters involved turbulence and difficulties maintaining altitude, no causal connection has been established. A preliminary report is expected within about 30 days, and the final investigation will likely take considerably longer.

On this day in space! Nov. 4, 2015: A rail-launched Super Strypi rocket carrying 13 baby satellites failed during its debut launch for the US Air Force

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Join The Club for quick access. Enter your email below and we'll send confirmation, and sign you up to our newsletter.

Latest space missions, discoveries and exploration updates.

Space.com's Sci-Fi Reader's Club. Read a sci-fi short story every month and join a virtual community of fellow science fiction fans!

Get full access to premium articles, exclusive features and a growing list of member rewards.

Super Strypi left the ground, but the rocket quickly spun out of control.

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

On Nov. 4, 2015, the United States Air Force planned to launch an experimental rocket for small satellites from the Pacific Missile Range Facility off Barking Sands, Kauai, Hawaii. The launch didn't according to plan.

For this mission, the Air Force was testing out the Super Strypi rocket. The rocket used a rail to help with the liftoff.

Super Strypi left the ground, but the rocket quickly spun out of control. When it crashed, it destroyed all of the tiny satellites riding aboard the rocket. The cause? Likely a problem with the first stage motor.

The mission lost 13 tiny satellites known as cubesats. Cubesats are a popular option for space missions, because they are small and cheap, and they often operate in swarms. After failing to launch this batch of cubesats, the Super Strypi program never recovered from its failure, and the Air Force never tried launching another one.

The Super Strypi rocket was a 21st century take on a 1960s rocket. It was designed to be a modern version of the three-stage Strypi sounding rocket used for for reentry vehicle and anti-missile target tests. Its three stages, powered by Aerojet Rocketdyne solid rocket motors, was designed to reach orbit, rather than suborbital trajectories common to most sounding rockets.

The U.S. Air Force hoped Super Strypi would be a new tool for quick-response launches based on national security needs. Altogether, it was designed to launch small payloads of up to 300 kilograms into space for a mere $15 million per mission.

The rocket's primary payload was the ORS-4 satellite, short for Operationally Responsive Space 4, which was built for the Air Force by engineers at the University of Hawaii (who nicknamed it HiakaSat). It was designed to capture Earth imagery and monitor the environment over the course of a year.

The 12 other payloads on Super Strypi included eight cubesats for NASA's Edison Demonstration of Smallsat Networks mission, and as well as four others for universities that had been added as secondary payloads.

Since the Super Strypi failure, neither the Air Force or Space Force has opted to use the rocket for a new mission. The Space Force has turned instead to commercial companies for fast-turnaround launch support.

In June of this year, the U.S. Space Force teamed up with Rocket Lab to launch the Victus Haze mission with just 17 hours of notice, a new record to demonstrate short-turnaround launch response.

On this day in space! Nov. 3, 1957: Laika the dog launches with Sputnik 2

Hanneke Weitering is a multimedia journalist in the Pacific Northwest reporting on the future of aviation at FutureFlight.aero and Aviation International News and was previously the Editor for Spaceflight and Astronomy news here at Space.com. As an editor with over 10 years of experience in science journalism she has previously written for Scholastic Classroom Magazines, MedPage Today and The Joint Institute for Computational Sciences at Oak Ridge National Laboratory. After studying physics at the University of Tennessee in her hometown of Knoxville, she earned her graduate degree in Science, Health and Environmental Reporting (SHERP) from New York University. Hanneke joined the Space.com team in 2016 as a staff writer and producer, covering topics including spaceflight and astronomy. She currently lives in Seattle, home of the Space Needle, with her cat and two snakes. In her spare time, Hanneke enjoys exploring the Rocky Mountains, basking in nature and looking for dark skies to gaze at the cosmos.

Politano: ‘Napoli cannot have this attitude, only Italians take responsibility’

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Matteo Politano seemed to launch a broadside at his Napoli teammates after the 4-1 defeat to FC Porto, noting it was ‘just us Italians’ who took responsibility for their difficulties.

The Partenopei had started quite strong at the Estadio do Dragao this evening, despite having a decimated squad with 10 players out between injury and ineligibility, leaving only three senior outfield figures on the bench.

The Jan Bednarek header on a corner was cancelled out by Scott McTominay, but they crumbled in the final 20 minutes to Gabriel Veiga’s counter-attack, another free header from Jakub Kiwior and the late Samu penalty with Rafa Marin red card.

When Politano was the first to speak after the match, Sky Sport Italia noted it was ‘always you’ who comes to represent the squad in the media room.

“It would be nice if a few other teammates came here to give an explanation and not just us Italians, but never mind,” replied Politano with what could only be described as a bitter laugh.

Napoli had hoped for better after the 2-0 Serie A defeat to Juventus at the weekend, but have now lost three out of four Champions League matches.

“We didn’t see the progress we wanted. The first half had been a good approach, we had equalised and created a few more chances that we could’ve done better, but we conceded two goals from corners and they hit the crossbar twice, all from the same situations, so there’s not a lot to say there,” added Politano.

“The attitude is one that a team like ours cannot have at this level.”

Considering his comment about only the Italians stepping up, does Politano feel there is a lack of unity at Napoli, or that Max Allegri is losing control of the side?

“I was not attacking anyone, we have a squad that is struggling, but we need to come together and think about this next game before the break for international duty. Hopefully we can recover a few players during that spell and then our season can really begin.”

It is a recurring theme that Napoli tend to crumble in the last 30 minutes of matches, both in Serie A and Europe, so is it purely a physical problem?

“I don’t know, we have asked ourselves that too. At times, it is not even a tactical issue, as we are all behind the ball, but we lack the determination and sharpness, we don’t read situations well or sense danger,” added Politano.

“In the Champions League and Serie A, the moment you leave the slightest bit of space, they punish you.”

Speaking to Amazon Prime Video Italia, Politano also added that “losing 2-1 and losing 4-1 is a big difference. We melted at the second goal and we need to take a good look at ourselves.”

Sony Promises Some Players Up To $400 To Trade In Their Used PS5s

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

In normal times, console makers regularly cut the price of their latest hardware in order to boost sales and increase revenue. As the cost of manufacturing a new console comes down, they’d drop the price to reach consumers who might not have previously considered buying one. They certainly weren’t begging existing players to trade in their machines for big refunds.

But that’s basically what Sony was doing in a recent alert that went out to some current PS5 owners. The alert, titled “Trade in your PS5 console for up to $400,” went out on November 2, and a screenshot of it was shared on the gaming forum ResetEra. “Enjoy simple, hassle-free service if you choose to trade in with Back Market,” the announcement read. “If you’re ready to sell your console, you could soon receive a cash payment into your nominated bank account. Find out how much you could get today.”

The deal seemed surprisingly good, even if getting the full $400 did require sending in two DualSense controllers rather than just one. That’s the price the all-digital PS5 launched at in 2020. Instead of going down, the official price tag has ratcheted up to $600. Used PS5s sold on places like eBay are currently going for around $435. Rarely do you see third-party refurbishment offers get anywhere close to lone seller price.

Xbox has been similarly aggressive, pinging players who haven’t turned on their Xbox Series X/S consoles in a while with offers of up to $375 to trade them in. That’s one strategy for getting around dwindling supplies and getting existing hardware into the hands of people who will actually buy and play new games on it. In the lead-up to Grand Theft Auto 6, both companies have every incentive to bolster inventory and limit the number of consoles sitting out there in the wild that are just collecting dust.

And if you happen to be in the market to get rid of your PS5 Pro, well, you’re in luck. They’ve become so hard to get that GameStop is currently selling used ones for up to $1,400. Don’t trade it in to the meme stock store chain though, obviously. You can get a lot more if you just sell it direct.

Where the Races Got Personal, These Democrats Came Out on Top

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

When, in late October, Ohio’s GOP gubernatorial candidate Vivek Ramaswamy ran an ad featuring Democratic opponent Amy Acton’s estranged mother—who stood by Acton’s stepfather after he was found guilty of raping Acton when she was 12—it seemed that political campaigning had reached a dramatic new low.

Ramaswamy’s ad was widely condemned, and on Tuesday, he lost the race for governor to Acton. “The healing begins today,” she said as she became Ohio’s first-ever female governor-elect.

Indeed, the victories that made up last night’s blue wave felt like a riposte to a range of Republican transgressions. Democrat Allie Phillips, who flipped a key House seat in Tennessee’s District 75, was motivated to run after her state’s near-total abortion ban prevented her from terminating a wanted but nonviable pregnancy in 2023. In Michigan, meanwhile, Democrat Abdul El-Sayed became the country’s first Muslim senator just over a week after President Donald Trump referred to him as a “jihadist” who was “on the side of terrorists,” doubling down on the Islamophobic attacks that dogged El-Sayed throughout his campaign.

When El-Sayed delivered his victory speech on Tuesday night, he wasted no time rebuking that hateful rhetoric, telling supporters: “Up until right about now, you might have noticed, I always say ‘Inshallah,’ or ‘God willing,’ whenever the outcome of this race was mentioned. But today, tonight, we can turn that ‘Inshallah’ into an ‘Alhamdulillah.’ For the right-wing ops, that just means ‘Praise be to God.’”

The queer community, too, had a lot to celebrate on Tuesday. Chris Pappas of New Hampshire became the country’s first openly gay senator, and at least three trans people were elected to public office: Allison Montgomery, who became the first openly transgender person ever to join the Alabama Legislature; Joanna Whaley, the first trans person elected to Michigan’s state legislature; and Washington State’s Jaelynn Scott, the nation’s first Black trans state lawmaker.

Given that few groups have been targeted by Republicans than trans people, these victories sent a strong message. As Scott put it in an interview, “This is not just about identity politics here in Washington. It’s actually about, in the face of these anti-trans initiatives that they’re pushing in our state and coming after our communities, that we’re actually throwing the finger at them.”

Hopecore Abounded at DSA Los Angeles’s Election-Night Watch Party

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

To quote Lana Del Rey, hope is a dangerous thing for a woman like me—specifically, a card-carrying member of the Democratic Socialists of America—to have. I’ve been volunteering for progressive candidates since I was 14 years old and haven’t missed a local or national election since I turned 18, but the reality of living in a democracy is that it’s a losing game to pin your hopes on any one politician. Even if they don’t personally let you down, the system they’re forced to operate within almost inevitably will.

Still, when New York’s 2025 mayoral race was called for Democratic Socialist Zohran Mamdani—someone I’d met in 2021, as he came off of a successful two-week hunger strike with the New York Taxi Workers Alliance—it made me feel like anything, or at least something, was possible. And a similar aura permeated the election-night watch party thrown by DSA Los Angeles at the Regent Theater on Tuesday, as members gathered to see the blue wave roll in.

I’m as sick of socialist jokes as the next leftist, but it has to be said: the crowd was largely young and stick-and-poked, their tote bags swinging in the early November breeze. A giant screen at the front of the space flipped through the election coverage on various channels (“Play Bravo!” one wit shouted through cupped hands) as we knocked back margaritas from the bar. Later, we listened to host Jamie Loftus and other leaders tout the six DSA candidates elected to the House that night—among other major wins—and thank the crowd for all their canvassing. I’d meant to stop by another election party at the Silverlake Lounge, but on the off chance that LA’s mayoral race was called for Nithya Raman, herself a DSA member, I thought that I should party with the people who would both celebrate her victory and work to push her further left from Day 1 of her tenure. (At the time of writing—nearly 24 hours later—her race against Karen Bass has still not officially been called.)

As the night went on, I spotted a group of cute, bearded, baseball-cap-clad guys reading funny election tweets aloud to each other while we waited for LA’s newly elected city attorney, Marissa Roy, to take the stage. (Soon hearing her inveigh against local ICE raids and lead the crowd in a chant of “Sue the landlords!” made me especially happy I’d stuck around.) Later, I found myself just outside the Regent, sharing a Parliament with a clutch of other party attendees who commiserated about falling behind on DSA dues. This called to mind for me the old joke about being able to identify the undercover FBI informants in the Students for a Democratic Society by the fact that they actually paid up every month.

In that moment, I allowed myself to sink fully into the beauty of being surrounded by hundreds of people with a shared vision not only for Los Angeles, but also the rest of the country, and who were ready to work for it. Or, as one attendee put it: “We’re libbing out tonight, but we’re organizing tomorrow.”

Snap has more than 30 million paid subscribers across Snapchat+ and other plans

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Power users seem more than willing to pay for AI-powered effects.

More than 30 million power users are paying for one of Snap's subscription plans, including Snapchat+ and its ad-free "platinum" tier. The company announced the milestone as part of its third-quarter earnings, saying that its non-advertising "other" revenue grew 84 percent compared with last year.

Snap introduced Snapchat+, which offers users bonus features like the ability to see which friends re-watched your Story, in 2022. Since then, the company has added Lens+, a subscription for AI-powered AR effects; Platinum, which eliminates advertising; and paid storage for the app's Memory feature.

Subscriptions were a bright spot for Snap. Spiegel said that advertising revenue was growing "below our ambitions" and the company only added 2 million new daily users since last quarter. Snap said that it would focus on reporting monthly active users rather than daily users, beginning in 2027. Snapchat currently has 977 million monthly users.

The company also warned that its legal challenges and "increased regulatory scrutiny on youth-related issues" could affect its prospects. The company is dealing with numerous lawsuits over alleged safety issues and state attorneys general have pushed for Snap to adopt additional safety features following Meta's high-profile settlement.

Arsenal boss Mikel Arteta reveals concerns with Champions League win over Slavia Prague

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Your weekly briefing on Arsenal, featuring team news and expert analysis from Matt Verri

I would like to be emailed about offers, event and updates from Evening Standard. Read our privacy notice.

Mikel Arteta admitted he was "very concerned" by Arsenal's second-half performance against Slavia Prague, despite the fact his side picked up all three points.

The Gunners were cruising at half-time of the Champions League clash, leading 2-0 through goals from Noni Madueke and Viktor Gyokeres.

It could have been an even bigger advantage, too, with Mikel Merino and Eberechi Eze both having good chances to get on the scoresheet themselves.

However, there was then a significant drop-off after the break. Arsenal were sloppy on the ball and there was an air of complacency to how they approached the second period.

Wiktor Nowak scored for Slavia to set up a nervy finish heading into stoppage-time but Arsenal just about held firm.

"Two very different halves," Arteta said. “The first one, we played a really high level. Exceptional at times I would say. The scoreline should reflect three or fouls goals.

"We didn't do it and at half-time I told them. They are a team with a huge spirit that will keep running, keep believing. They will become even more chaotic.

"We didn't control that at all. Right from the beginning of the first half we were on the back foot. Very sloppy. We gave so many balls away, so many bad clearances, we didn't keep the ball or the structure at all.

"Then you have to suffer because the teams in the Champions League are really good. A lot to learn."

Slavia registered 17 shots in the match, five more than Arsenal, and there was some chaotic moments at the back for the visitors.

Michelle Parker Murder Case: Timeline of Investigation Into Her Disappearance

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Nearly 15 years after Michelle Parker vanished the same day a pretaped episode of The People’s Court featuring her and her ex-fiancé aired on TV, prosecutors have formally charged Dale Smith Jr. with her murder.

Smith, 55, was arrested in Orlando, Florida, on Wednesday, October 14, on a second-degree murder charge in connection with Parker’s 2011 disappearance. Prosecutors then filed a formal charge in Orange County Circuit Court on Tuesday, November 3, according to a criminal information viewed by Us.

The filing alleges Smith “by an act imminently dangerous to another, and evincing a depraved mind regardless of human life, did kill Michelle Parker” on or around November 17, 2011.

Smith pleaded not guilty on October 15, records show. His defense attorney Rajan Joshi did not immediately return a request for comment from Us on Wednesday, November 4.

Parker, 33, was a mother of three children, including twins she shared with Smith. On November 17, 2011, a pretaped episode of The People’s Court featuring the former couple aired on TV.

The episode centered on Dale suing Parker over an engagement ring he claimed she had tossed off a balcony.

Parker accused Dale of infidelity on the show and described him as “pretty malicious and vindictive.”

“He’s cheated on me constantly and I’ve always tried to keep it together for the kids,” she said in court. “He said that he wanted his ring, so I took it off, and I threw it at him.”

The same day the episode aired, Parker dropped her then-3-year-old twins off at Smith’s condo and planned to run errands before her bartending shift. She never arrived at work.

Her sister reported her missing to the Orlando Police Department that day, according to an affidavit unsealed on October 26 and obtained by Us. Her body has never been located.

Parker’s Hummer was found abandoned at an Orlando mall the day after she went missing, according to a 2012 report from the Palm Beach Post. A few weeks later, her iPhone was recovered underwater near the Nela Bridge in Belle Isle, south of Orlando.

Smith was initially named as a suspect. In an interview with police on November 18, 2011, he claimed he “had no idea” where Parker was after she dropped off the twins. He also told investigators he was not involved in her disappearance.

Smith confirmed he had watched The People’s Court episode when asked, and told police he believed Parker “was embarrassed” by it, the affidavit states.

“He stated she was just laying low and had no idea if anything happened to her at all,” the filing continued, adding that “he wanted her to come home safe because his kids need their mom.”

Following the interview, Smith allowed investigators to photograph scratches and abrasions now suspected to be defensive wounds caused by Parker, according to the affidavit, which includes the images

The Orlando Police Department announced on October 14 that Smith had been arrested and charged with second-degree murder in relation to Parker’s disappearance. The department also posted a video on social media showing Smith being led out of a building in handcuffs by the Fugitive Unit and SWAT Team.

“This arrest follows years of investigative work and a renewed, focused effort over the past year,” Orlando police said in a news release. “An expanded investigative team reexamined evidence and used advances in digital forensics to uncover and analyze additional evidence that moved this case forward.”

Though Parker’s remains have never been found, police said “all evidence leads to Dale Smith Jr. as the person responsible for Michelle’s murder,” police said.

One day after the arrest, Parker’s mother, Yvonne Stewart, told WESH she was not surprised by the development.

“I knew,” Stewart said on Thursday, October 15. “We all knew. Yeah, we all knew.”

She added, “They told me they arrested Dale Smith on second-degree murder charges for Michelle Parker, and it’s like the happiest day because she deserves this.”

Stewart also made a direct appeal: “I would love for [Smith] to tell us where she is.”

Joshi, Smith’s attorney since 2011, said his client “maintains his innocence, and we’re going to go forward with defending him,” noting that “the arrest came out of nowhere” and no one was “expecting it.”

“Whatever evidence they retested is something we want to take a look at,” Joshi said. “I plan to go through the case with a fine-tooth comb, leave no stones unturned and talk to every witness that there is, depose them and get down to it.”

In a separate interview with USA Today on October 15, Joshi said he visited Smith in jail. “That’s the mother of his child,” he told the outlet. “We want to see justice done for Michelle Parker and her family. We just don’t want the wrong guy convicted.”

According to the affidavit detailing the evidence that led to Smith’s arrest, Police said Smith claimed Parker was only at his home for about 10 minutes, but enhanced analysis of cellphone location data revealed she was there for over an hour.

Investigators also believe that after Smith allegedly killed Parker, he sent a text message to her brother from her phone saying she was at a shopping center — though her phone was actually more than 10 miles away at that point. Police believe Parker was already dead when the text was sent.

The affidavit states witnesses claimed Smith was abusive, and one friend told police Smith told Parker her “time is coming” during a fight. Earlier this year, on March 10, an investigator met with one of Parker’s close friends, who said Parker was worried about seeing Smith on November 17, 2011, when The People’s Court episode was going to air.

“She described the victim as a ‘nervous wreck,’” the affidavit states. “She said the judge on the show was very insulting and she knew it would not sit well with the suspect because everyone would see it. The victim found it embarrassing.”

The affidavit also included texts the pair exchanged in the weeks before Parker’s disappearance. In one message, Parker threatened to take custody of the twins.

“Mourn for ur kids too! U lost em,” Parker allegedly wrote two weeks before she died. “They’ll b goin with me a**hole.”

Investigators suspect Smith fatally strangled Parker, per the affidavit.

“It is believed the suspect choked the victim to death, likely resulting in the scratches and abrasions observed on him while the victim fought back,” the affidavit states.

Smith is seeking a specialized bond hearing after he was ordered to be held without bond, per a motion filed November 3.

The filing argues he’s “entitled to due process and the presumption of innocence in this case” and claims prosecutors cannot link him to Parker’s murder. The motion also argues for his immediate release on monetary bond. He remains in custody at the Orange County Jail.

Champions League power rankings: PSG remain a joke – but who is catching them?

📅 2026-11-04  |  Read Source →

Please refresh the page or navigate to another page on the site to be automatically logged inPlease refresh your browser to be logged in

Paris Saint-Germain and Real Madrid are the only teams to still have 100 per cent records after Liverpool’s defeat at Fenerbahce

Want to bookmark your favourite articles and stories to read or reference later? Start your Independent Membership today.

It was largely business as usual in matchweek four of the Champions League as Paris Saint-Germain and Barcelona kept on scoring and Arsenal and Bayern Munich returned to winning ways while remaining unbeaten.

But the story of the round belonged to Como, who made history by beating Lens 9-1 away from home and equalling the record for the most goals scored in a single Champions League game. Cesc Fabregas’s impressive side now sit fourth in the league phase standings, behind PSG, Real Madrid and Arsenal.

Meanwhile, there was a frustrating defeat for Liverpool at Fenerbahce, but Manchester United produced a stunning comeback to beat Roma and potentially save Michael Carrick’s job, with Matheus Cunha scoring twice on a remarkable night at Old Trafford.

We compiled our 36-team power rankings by combining team’s previous performance in the Champions League, their results in the competition so far, and the likelihood of them going all the way this season.

Agree or disagree with our standings? Let us know and have your say in the comments below.

The reigning champions are making a mockery of suggestions that they are slow starters in the Champions League. Luis Enrique’s back-to-back champions finished outside the top eight in both of their previous successes but have started this campaign on fire in Europe. PSG were 4-0 up after just 17 minutes at Villarreal, and that was without Ousmane Dembele and Khvicha Kvaratskhelia. The French champions will be without the brilliant Georgian for the rest of the season after he suffered a ruptured ACL but they remain the team to beat.

Defeats to Paris Saint-Germain in the Champions League and Real Madrid in LaLiga ended their winning start under Hansi Flick and perhaps raised the same concerns over whether their defence holds up come the knockout stages. But Lamine Yamal is arguably the world’s best player on current form and Raphinha isn’t too bad, either. The Brazilian made it an astonishing 19 goals and three assists in just 13 matches across LaLiga and the Champions League with his double in the 3-0 win over Aston Villa at Camp Nou.

Mikel Arteta’s got the result they needed in Prague, even if a difficult second-half performance made it a bit of a struggle. An entertaining draw last time out at Bayern Munich, who have such a strong home record, gave a good account of whether the Premier League champions can take the next step against a heavyweight side in the business end of the Champions League.

Bayern returned to winning ways with a professional win against Atletico Madrid – and the big surprise was that Harry Kane was not on the scoresheet. The Ballon d’Or holder’s 10-match scoring run in the Champions League came to an end at the Metropolitano, missing the chance to equal Cristiano Ronaldo’s record. But Bayern rolled on.

Perhaps chaos is the new normal under Jose Mourinho. Real Madrid trailed late on in Athens but Kylian Mbappe equalised before Fede Valverde scored a stunning winner in stoppage time. Jude Bellingham also scored an outrageous flick. Somehow with maximum points, despite trailing Roma 3-1 at the Stadio Olimpico, and Inter and RB Leipzig spurning chances to take the lead at the Bernabeu, the 15-time Champions League winners continue to find a way.

Back-to-back defeats in the Premier League have the shine off an impressive start for Enzo Maresca’s side, while uncertainty off the pitch remains as well. But a battling win at RB Leipzig, with a customary Erling Haaland goal, was a good response, even if City have now gone seven games without a clean sheet. Next up: the return of Kevin De Bruyne with Napoli, followed by a mouthwatering trip to Barcelona.

Liverpool have conceded three goals in three consecutive games in the Champions League or European Cup for the first time and the manner of their “messy” collapse against Fenerbahce was a concern. The nature of Liverpool’s squad means Andoni Iraola has needed to rotate in Champions League but Liverpool’s performance in Istanbul underlined the task he faces. In-form Club Brugge can’t be taken lightly after the international break.

Despite defeat in the Camp Nou, you get the feeling that Unai Emery’s new-look Aston Villa will have learned a lot from their meeting with Barcelona. They had moments of threat, too. Bright showings from the refreshed attacking line-up of Nicolas Jackson, Johan Manzambi, Ibrahim Mbaye and Alysson have helped the Europa League winners retaining their identity under Emery despite the huge squad turnover in the summer.

After starting with back-to-back defeats, how badly Inter needed to respond with back-to-back wins. A comfortable win at Feyenoord also came after Saturday’s Milan derby defeat, with Pio Esposito stepping up amid Lautaro Martinez’s continued absence.

WOW. If Como announced themselves by thrashing Manchester United, the Champions League debutants stole the show this week with a NINE-goal dismantling of poor Lens.
